  52 pages (p031 under-split, p045 over-split) — and both are recall-plateau on
  rules already in the prompt, not prompt gaps. The rest of your edits were
  cosmetic box padding. So on typed letters, it's highly automated. I also

● The review workflow is running in the background (w8dxot79q, run
  wf_190a9bf0-8b2) — ~29 agents across 5 phases. Here's the shape of what's
  underway and what I'll do with it:

  Running now (~10–20 min):
  1. Agreement — 10 agents, one per confusable axis (archv vs src date, origin
  vs location, mottos/blessings by position, recipient vs greeting,
  doc-segmentation, connections-by-page-type, …). Each views the annotated
  overlays and audits where your labels disagree with each other or with the
  stated rule.
  2. Verify — an independent skeptic re-checks each axis's claims from the
  images (kills plausible-but-wrong findings; flags missed ones).
  3. Coverage — 6 agents classify each uncovered_ink miss as real miss / 
  gold-also-skips / detector noise, proposing only non-overfit fixes.
  4. Research — web best-practices (annotation-agreement, few-shot consistency,
  no-overfit recall) + a corpus-vs-portable teardown of this flow.
  5. Synthesis — collapses it into: acceptable-range table,
  relabel/exclude/rehabilitate lists, a non-overfit coverage plan, and
  generalization principles.

  Then I'll write the findings into LABEL_REVIEW.md, checkpoint, apply the
  high-confidence non-overfit changes, re-run Appendix_1, and re-measure
  coverage + agreement — looping until it's solid, checkpointing before each
  mutation so everything stays revertible.

  You can watch live with /workflows. I'll pick this up automatically when it
  finishes and report what came back — no need to babysit it.

      1240 +# ── Coverage backstop (general, content-driven recall recove
           +ry) ───────────────                                          
      1241 +                                                             
      1242 +BACKSTOP_PROMPT = """\                                       
      1243 +You previously labeled this scanned archival page, but an aut
           +omatic coverage check found written TEXT that no polygon encl
           +oses. Your job now is ONLY to box that missing text — do NOT 
           +relabel, move, or re-emit anything already correct.          
      1244 +                                                             
      1245 +These regions (normalized [0,1000]; each given as top-left (x
           +0,y0) to bottom-right (x1,y1)) currently contain UNBOXED ink:
      1246 +{regions}                                                    
      1247 +                                                             
      1248 +For EACH region that holds written source/letter/archival tex
           +t, emit a polygon (EXACTLY 4 vertices, coordinates in [0,1000
           +]) tightly bounding that text, tagged with its correct catego
           +ry. A region that is a wrapped continuation or trailing remai
           +nder of adjacent text should still get a box with the best-fi
           +t category of that adjacent text. Do NOT box non-text marks (
           +ink stamps, library/call numbers, punch holes, stray specks) 
           +— skip those regions. Return the SAME schema; include ONLY th
           +e new boxes; assign each to the document (doc_1, doc_2, ...) 
           +it belongs to."""                                            
      1249 +                                                             
      1250 +                                                             
      1251 +def build_backstop_prompt_parts(target_image: Image.Image, re
           +gions: list[dict]) -> list:                                  
      1252 +    """Prompt the model to box only the regions a coverage ch
           +eck left uncovered."""                                       
      1253 +    cats = "\n".join(f"  - {cat}: {CATEGORY_DESCRIPTIONS[cat]
           +}" for cat in CATEGORIES)                                    
      1254 +    reg = "\n".join(f"  - ({r['x0']},{r['y0']}) to ({r['x1']}
           +,{r['y1']})" for r in regions)                               
      1255 +    return [                                                 
      1256 +        "CATEGORIES:\n" + cats,                              
      1257 +        BACKSTOP_PROMPT.format(regions=reg),                 
      1258 +        "PAGE (already partially labeled):",                 
      1259 +        target_image,                                        
      1260 +    ]                                                        
      1261 +                                                             
      1262 +                                                             
      1263 +def _merge_added_boxes(documents: dict, new_docs: dict) -> in
           +t:                                                           
      1264 +    """Add boxes from new_docs into the existing documents, a
           +ssigning each to the                                         
      1265 +    document whose vertical span contains it (nearest otherwi
           +se). Never creates a                                         
      1266 +    new document, so num_documents is unchanged. Returns the 
           +number of boxes added."""                                    
      1267 +    if not documents:                                        
      1268 +        return 0                                             
      1269 +    spans = {}                                               
      1270 +    for dname, doc in documents.items():                     
      1271 +        ys = [v for _cat, polys in doc.items() for b in polys
      1272 +              for v in (_bbox_of(b["vertices"])[1], _bbox_of(
           +b["vertices"])[3])]                                          
      1273 +        if ys:                                               
      1274 +            spans[dname] = (min(ys), max(ys))                
      1275 +    default_doc = next(iter(documents))                      
      1276 +    added = 0                                                
      1277 +    for _dn, ndoc in new_docs.items():                       
      1278 +        for cat, polys in ndoc.items():                      
      1279 +            for b in polys:                                  
      1280 +                if not b.get("vertices"):                    
      1281 +                    continue                                 
      1282 +                bb = _bbox_of(b["vertices"])                 
      1283 +                yc = (bb[1] + bb[3]) / 2                     
      1284 +                tgt, best = default_doc, None                
      1285 +                for dname, (y0, y1) in spans.items():        
      1286 +                    dist = 0 if y0 <= yc <= y1 else min(abs(y
           +c - y0), abs(yc - y1))                                       
      1287 +                    if best is None or dist < best:          
      1288 +                        best, tgt = dist, dname              
      1289 +                documents[tgt].setdefault(cat, []).append(b) 
      1290 +                added += 1                                   
      1291 +    return added                                             
      1292 +                                                             
      1293 +                                                             
      1294 +def coverage_backstop(                                       
      1295 +    documents:   dict,                                       
      1296 +    page_gray:   Image.Image,                                
      1297 +    target_img:  Image.Image,                                
      1298 +    client:      genai.Client,                               
      1299 +    model_name:  str,                                        
      1300 +    page_width:  int,                                        
      1301 +    page_height: int,                                        
      1302 +) -> tuple[int, int, int]:                                   
      1303 +    """Second-pass recall recovery: re-prompt the model to bo
           +x any ink its OWN                                            
      1304 +    output left uncovered. General and content-driven — fires
           + only where real ink                                         
      1305 +    is unboxed, with zero page-specific knowledge, and only A
           +DDS boxes (existing                                          
      1306 +    labels and num_documents are never touched). Returns (n_a
           +dded, in_tok, out_tok)."""                                   
      1307 +    import qa_report  # lazy: qa_report imports this module a
           +t load time                                                  
      1308 +    ink = qa_report._ink_mask(page_gray)                     
      1309 +    W, H = page_gray.size                                    
      1310 +    flags = qa_report.check_uncovered_ink(documents, ink, W, 
           +H)                                                           
      1311 +    if not flags:                                            
      1312 +        return 0, 0, 0                                       
      1313 +    regions = [{                                             
      1314 +        "x0": round(f["bbox"][0] / W * NORM_RANGE), "y0": rou
           +nd(f["bbox"][1] / H * NORM_RANGE),                           
      1315 +        "x1": round(f["bbox"][2] / W * NORM_RANGE), "y1": rou
           +nd(f["bbox"][3] / H * NORM_RANGE),                           
      1316 +    } for f in flags]                                        
      1317 +    parts = build_backstop_prompt_parts(target_img, regions) 
      1318 +    resp, in_tok, out_tok = call_gemini(client, model_name, p
           +arts)                                                        
      1319 +    new_docs = _scale_response_to_original(resp, 0, 0, page_w
           +idth, page_height)                                           
      1320 +    snap_all_polygons(new_docs, page_gray)   # snap ONLY the 
           +new boxes                                                    
      1321 +    added = _merge_added_boxes(documents, new_docs)          
      1322 +    return added, in_tok, out_tok

● This is the key finding, and it confirms your intuition: of the 16 real
  misses, 14 were "box too short" (the ink abuts an existing box) and only 2 
  were genuine orphan regions needing a new label. So the distinction you're
  drawing is exactly the dominant case.

  Q1 — Are the two cases separated and handled? Detected-ish, but handled: no.

  Detection (qa_report) does have two flag types — uncovered_ink (ink in no box)
  and clipped_edge (text just outside a box edge). But the split is by detector
  geometry, not by intended fix, and there's a trap: a box that's short by a 
  whole line lands in uncovered_ink, not clipped_edge (the clip detector needs
  ink reaching the box's own edge). That's why the no-backstop run showed 16
  uncovered_ink / 1 clipped_edge even though 14 were really "extend" cases.

  Handling — and this is the gap you're pointing at — does not distinguish at
  all:
  - The backstop I built (_merge_added_boxes) is append-only: it always drops a
  new box. On ~14/16 cases it put a separate sliver box right under the box that
  should have just grown.
  - The editor's Accept does the same — adds a new rectangle of whatever label
  is currently active.

  Coverage + label come out right (so it passed your "covered + labelled" bar),
  but the topology is wrong — a paragraph gets fragmented instead of extended,
  and there's a real risk the re-prompt labels a wrapped continuation as a
  different category. So: worth fixing properly. The fix is adjacency-aware — if
  the uncovered ink abuts a box, grow that box's edge; only re-prompt for a new
  box on true orphans.

  Q2 — The review queue is bare, and I can make it carry this distinction.

  The code is a plain Listbox + Go To / Accept / Dismiss / Reload. Accept only
  fixes uncovered_ink, adds a new box of the active label (you have to
  pre-select the category — clunky), everything else says "no auto-fix, do it by
  hand," and dismissals vanish on reload. Concrete upgrades, tied to Q1:
  - Two actions: Extend nearest box and Add new box — with the category
  auto-suggested (from the abutting box / the model), not whatever's active.
  - Keyboard-driven (↑↓ through flags, Enter=apply, D=dismiss) with the canvas
  auto-centering/zooming on the selected flag, and a ghost preview of the
  proposed box.
  - Persist dismissals, group by type/severity, mark which are auto-fixable.

  Q3 — Roll label-agreement into the prompt? Partially — and here's the pushback
  you invited.

  The canonical rules are already in the prompt. The acceptable-range table is
  mostly a scoring tolerance (don't penalize either reading) — and dumping "both
  X and Y are fine" into the prompt would raise variance: for labeling you want
  a consistent default, not license to flip-flop. (The review's own principle
  #3: medium-confidence prompt edits overcorrected twice here.)

  What does help is the opposite of tolerance — a single tie-break default for
  each genuinely contradictory zone. Concretely the 2 confirmed prompt-gaps:
  opening-doxology greeting-vs-other gated on an intervening dateline, and
  non-US modern addresses counting as archv_possessor. Those resolve real
  contradictions → more consistency. Roll those in, re-validate with the
  held-out test. Not the full tolerance bands.

  So: agree it helps, but as defaults/tie-breaks, not as "ranges."

  Which should I build?

  (both are automatic, so human review still shrinks).

  What I built (both things you selected)

  Extend-vs-new recognition — and the key insight your question forced out. My
  first instinct (extend the geometrically-nearest box) failed 7/16 when
  validated against your gold: on page_033 it would inherit a wrong-category
  neighbor (src_date, archv_commentary) for what is really one src_greeting
  block. So the recognizer is now category-safe:

  ▎ Category comes from the model (re-prompted fresh with full-page context); 
  ▎ geometry only decides topology — a box that abuts an existing same-category 
  ▎ box extends it; otherwise it's a new box; adjacent same-category additions 
  ▎ coalesce.

  This can't mislabel by construction. Re-validated against gold: 16/16 labels 
  correct. page_022's 4 continuation lines now extend the body (0 new boxes);
  page_033's missing greeting block becomes new src_greeting boxes.

  - Backstop (auto_labeler.py): _merge_added_boxes rewritten; the log now reads
  extended X, added Y so the two cases are visible. Verified by deterministic
  simulation + a live page_033 run (uncovered 6→0, doc-count untouched).
  - Editor (normalized_editor.py): an Extend button + a per-flag hint in the
  queue — abuts src_content → Extend vs orphan → Add box. One click grows the
  right box for the dominant "too short" case; Add box stays for genuine
  orphans. No more pre-selecting the label type and hoping.

  On Q3 (label-agreement → prompt): you didn't select it, so I left it — my
  recommendation stands that only the 2 tie-break defaults belong in the prompt,
  not the tolerance ranges (which would raise variance). It's queued whenever
  you want it.

● All verified and logged. Here's the full picture on your three questions:

  1. How extreme is the overlap? Mostly not.

  Measured by the actual ink in each shared region
  (rerun_compare/overlap_severity.py):

  ┌────────────┬───────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │            │ count │                     what it is                     │
  ├────────────┼───────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ whitespace │ 0     │ —                                                  │
  ├────────────┼───────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ partial    │ 19    │ thin ink band — clipped character tops/bottoms; a  │
  │            │       │ crop grabs a sliver, not a word                    │
  ├────────────┼───────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ full-word  │ 11    │ shared region holds ≥½ a text line of ink          │
  ├────────────┼───────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ └          │       │ …and it's a different category                     │
  │ cross-role │ 6     │ (content+farewell/signature/date) — the real       │
  │            │       │ swallow                                            │
  └────────────┴───────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So the honest answer: the overlap is rarely extreme — 19/30 are exactly the
  partial-character case you described, and only 6 would actually pull a full
  foreign word into a crop.

  The detector now matches your rule: check_overlap_loose is ink-aware — it
  flags only when the shared region holds a full word belonging to a different 
  category or document box. Whitespace, partial-char, and same-role sub-line
  containment ("reasonable" containment) are no longer flagged. Overlap flags 
  went 30 → 6, and the editor queue inherits it (it reads qa's flags). And yes —
  your "if you notice a half-finished word or stray mark, weed it out"
  extraction-prompt instruction is exactly the right way to mop up the 19
  partials; that's why they're not worth flagging.

  2. Leakage — no, and now fixed.

  Straight answer: the re-run was not leak-free. select_few_shot filtered by
  volume but never held out the page being labeled, so 9 of 52 Appendix_1 pages 
  saw their own answer (the same issue I flagged in our first conversation but
  never fixed — my miss). The other 43 were clean, and the coverage/extend
  results hold on those, but you were right to check.

  Fixed: both pass-1 and pass-2 selection now take a target_page and exclude it
  — verified page_001 no longer appears in its own few-shot. Every future run is
  leak-free on all 52.

  Your call

  The current auto_labeled/Appendix_1 still carries the old leak on those 9
  pages. A fresh run (~$1, ~12 min) would be fully leak-free and show the
  refined overlap count volume-wide — want me to kick it off? Otherwise
  everything's committed to checkpoints 01–06, import-clean, and LABEL_REVIEW.md
  is current through Iter 5.

● Kicking off the leak-free re-run now, and I'll build the overlap-resolution
  pass while it runs.

  On folding overlaps into a pass — I think it's worth it, but only the safe 
  way: I won't blindly trim geometry (deciding whose word is in the overlap is
  the same ambiguity as extend-vs-new, and a wrong trim mislabels). The safe
  version uses the blank line between the two text blocks as the natural
  boundary — if there's a clear ink gap separating (say) the content's last line
  from the farewell, snap both boxes to that gap: overlap gone, each box keeps
  its own text, nothing uncovered. Where there's no clean gap, leave it flagged
  for you. Let me start the run, then build + test that deterministically.

● The gap approach was too narrow — it searched only the overlap span, but these
  are padding overlaps (a content box's bottom padding extends over the
  farewell/sig text below it, while content's own text ended above). So it
  safely skipped all 6 (0 new uncovered — the safety property held). The right
  fix: trim the box's padding back to its own text, using the blank line between
  the two text blocks. Let me rewrite with that, plus a
  revert-if-it-uncovers-anything safety net:

● Good instinct — "no flags" only means coverage + overlap are clean; QA can't
  see label/granularity issues. I rendered auto_labeled/Appendix_1/page_012 with
  category labels (/tmp/page_012_labels.png). Here's what I see:

  What's correct:
  - It's a continuation page of Fr. Solanus's near-death narrative (no
  greeting/recipient/date — starts mid-sentence). num_documents = 1 ✓
  - Full coverage — every paragraph, the closing, and the signature are boxed
  (matches uncovered_ink=0).
  - Categories are all right: the closing Praised be JESUS CHRIST…In the Sacred 
  Heart → src_farewell (valediction slot, above the sig) ✓, Fr. Solanus →
  src_signature ✓, and the body is src_content.

  What I do notice (and QA can't flag):
  The body is split into 5 separate src_content boxes (one per paragraph). But
  the scheme's own rule says a continuous-prose letter should be ONE src_content
  polygon spanning the whole body — per-paragraph boxes are reserved for
  multi-entry notebook pages (SYSTEM_PROMPT: "emit exactly ONE src_content 
  polygon… Per-block src_content polygons are ONLY for multi-entry 
  notebook/journal/ledger pages").

  So page_012 is over-segmented: 5 content boxes where the convention wants 1.

  How much does it matter? By your stated bar (covered + correctly labelled),
  it's benign — all 5 are correctly src_content and nothing's missed; for
  crop-and-extract it even works fine (5 chunks concatenate to the body). But it
  is a real deviation from your gold convention, and it's systematic — the
  model tends to box prose by paragraph. This is exactly the blind spot you
  suspected: no flags ≠ matches your labeling rules.

  If you want the one-box convention enforced, the cleanest fix is a "merge 
  adjacent same-document same-category src_content boxes" normalization pass
  (reusing the merge logic I already built for the backstop) — it'd collapse
  those 5 into 1 automatically, safely, on every letter. Want me to add that and
  check how many other pages are over-segmented this way?

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Found it. The uncovered text is:

  etc.

  One instance — the etc. at the end of paragraph 4 ("Charity knows no 
  evil....is not suspicious," etc.). It sits on its own at the left margin
  (~position 868,4030) and the paragraph's src_content box stops just above it,
  so it's enclosed by nothing.

  And here's the real problem you exposed: QA reported uncovered_ink=0 and was
  wrong. That region is 62px tall with ~2,524 ink pixels — but the detector's
  minimum thresholds are ~82px tall (UNCOVERED_MIN_H) and ~3,644 ink px
  (UNCOVERED_MIN_INK). etc. falls under both, so the detector silently drops it.
  So:

  - "uncovered_ink = 0 volume-wide" was overstated — short words/fragments slip
  under the size floor.
  - Worse, the backstop can't fix what QA never flags — it only re-prompts on
  flagged uncovered ink, so etc. was never even a candidate. The whole coverage
  guarantee has a small-fragment blind spot.

  This is exactly the failure you were sniffing out: clean flags ≠ clean page.

  Fix: lower UNCOVERED_MIN_H/UNCOVERED_MIN_INK so a real short word like etc.
  (2,524 px) is caught while specks/punctuation (<~few hundred px) still aren't
  — then re-run QA across all 52 pages to surface every sub-threshold miss, and
  let the backstop mop them up. Want me to do that and tell you how many more
  etc.-class misses are hiding volume-wide?

      763 +    def _merge_doc_into_previous(self):                       
      764 +        """Merge the CURRENT document into the previous one (d
          +oc_N -> doc_N-1).                                             
      765 +                                                              
      766 +        For pages over-split into documents (e.g. front/back o
          +f one card): all of                                           
      767 +        doc_N's boxes move into doc_N-1, every later document 
          +is renumbered down by                                         
      768 +        one, and connection {doc,...} references are remapped 
          +to match. Adjacent                                            
      769 +        merge only — to merge doc_2 and doc_3, select doc_3 an
          +d Merge ▲."""                                                 
      770 +        try:                                                  
      771 +            n = int(self.current_doc.split("_", 1)[1])        
      772 +        except (ValueError, IndexError):                      
      773 +            return                                            
      774 +        if n <= 1:                                            
      775 +            messagebox.showinfo("Merge docs", "doc_1 has no pr
          +evious document to merge into.")                              
      776 +            return                                            
      777 +        docs = self.page_data.get("documents", {})            
      778 +        src_name, dst_name = f"doc_{n}", f"doc_{n - 1}"       
      779 +        if src_name not in docs:                              
      780 +            return                                            
      781 +        if not messagebox.askyesno(                           
      782 +                "Merge docs",                                 
      783 +                f"Merge {src_name} into {dst_name}?\n\nAll of 
          +its boxes move into {dst_name} "                              
      784 +                f"and later documents are renumbered down by o
          +ne."):                                                        
      785 +            return                                            
      786 +        self._cancel_draw_if_active()                         
      787 +        # 1) move every polygon into the previous document    
      788 +        dst = docs.setdefault(dst_name, {})                   
      789 +        for cat, polys in (docs.get(src_name) or {}).items(): 
      790 +            if isinstance(polys, list) and polys:             
      791 +                dst.setdefault(cat, []).extend(polys)         
      792 +        del docs[src_name]                                    
      793 +        # 2) renumber all later documents down by one         
      794 +        total = int(self.page_data.get("num_documents", len(do
          +cs) + 1))                                                     
      795 +        rename = {src_name: dst_name}                         
      796 +        for i in range(n + 1, total + 1):                     
      797 +            old, new = f"doc_{i}", f"doc_{i - 1}"             
      798 +            if old in docs:                                   
      799 +                docs[new] = docs.pop(old)                     
      800 +                rename[old] = new                             
      801 +        # 3) remap connection doc references everywhere on the
          + page                                                         
      802 +        for doc in docs.values():                             
      803 +            if not isinstance(doc, dict):                     
      804 +                continue                                      
      805 +            for polys in doc.values():                        
      806 +                if not isinstance(polys, list):               
      807 +                    continue                                  
      808 +                for p in polys:                               
      809 +                    for cn in (p.get("connections") or []):   
      810 +                        if cn.get("doc") in rename:           
      811 +                            cn["doc"] = rename[cn["doc"]]     
      812 +        # 4) keep keys in doc_1..doc_N order for clean JSON   
      813 +        def _idx(k):                                          
      814 +            try:                                              
      815 +                return int(k.split("_", 1)[1])                
      816 +            except (ValueError, IndexError):                  
      817 +                return 999                                    
      818 +        self.page_data["documents"] = {k: docs[k] for k in sor
          +ted(docs, key=_idx)}                                          
      819 +        # 5) bookkeeping                                      
      820 +        self.page_data["num_documents"] = max(1, total - 1)   
      821 +        self.num_docs_var.set(self.page_data["num_documents"])
      822 +        self.current_doc = dst_name                           
      823 +        self.selected_polygon_idx = None                      
      824 +        self._rebuild_doc_buttons()                           
      825 +        self._draw_scene()                                    
      826 +        self._update_status()

● Built and verified. The merge works exactly as you specced:

  Test on page_031 (in-memory, nothing saved):
  - 3 docs {doc_1: 5, doc_2: 4, doc_3: 7} → 2 docs {doc_1: 9, doc_2: 7}
  - doc_2's boxes folded into doc_1, old doc_3 renumbered to doc_2,
  num_documents 3→2
  - Connection refs remapped (a synthetic edge pointing at doc_3 correctly
  became doc_2) — that was the one landmine, since connections store {doc, type,
  id}
  - Selection lands on the merged doc; the doc buttons rebuild

  How to use it: in the editor, select the doc you want to fold upward (e.g.
  doc_2 = back of card), click Merge ▲ (bottom of the Documents panel), confirm.
  Saves to reviewed/ on page-change/exit as usual. To merge doc_2+doc_3, select
  doc_3 and Merge ▲ — adjacent-only, per your simplifying assumption.

  Two consistency notes since this is a convention change (front/back of one
  card = one document):

  1. labeled_examples/Appendix_1/page_031 is a separate copy — it's one of the
  promoted multi-doc few-shot examples, still 3 docs. If you merge the reviewed
  gold but leave the few-shot copy at 3, the model will keep being taught to
  split front/back. After you do the merge in the editor, say the word and I'll
  sync the few-shot copy (or re-promote it from reviewed).
  2. The deferred page_028 num_documents 1→3 edit was justified by "must match
  page_031 = 3" — under your new convention that becomes 1→2, so good thing we
  deferred it.

  If front/back-of-card keeps tripping the model on future volumes, a one-line
  prompt tie-break ("opposite sides of the same physical card are ONE document")
  would encode it — but let's see it misbehave first before adding rules.

● Done and logged. The lever:

  - MAX_CONCURRENCY = 4 global at the top of auto_labeler.py, overridable
  per-run with --concurrency N (1 = old serial behavior)
  - Backoff strengthened for the rate-limit reality of parallel calls: 6
  attempts with jittered exponential backoff up to 60s (wait_random_exponential
  — jitter matters when 4 workers hit a 429 simultaneously, otherwise they retry
  in lockstep and collide again)
  - --delay repurposed as a submission stagger — a gentle ramp so the pool
  doesn't thundering-herd the API at t=0
  - Determinism preserved (playbook §3.9): few-shot selection still runs
  sequentially in the main thread before dispatch, so a seed-42 run picks
  identical examples at any concurrency; workers only do the API/render/write
  work, and each page writes to its own directory so there's nothing shared to
  corrupt

  Appendix_3 estimate is now ~3–4 min (47 pages) instead of ~11. Everything from
  the readiness check still stands — still GO whenever you say the word, and if
  Gemini's per-key limits turn out generous you can try --concurrency 6-8 on a
  later volume; the backoff makes over-shooting safe (it degrades to waiting,
  not failures).

● Not premature at all — the review caught one genuine landmine. Full findings:

  🔴 Found & fixed: an OOM bug waiting for the big volumes

  render_page cached every full-resolution render unbounded (~110MB each).
  Appendix_1's 52 pages quietly held ~5GB and "worked" — Volume_2's 373 pages 
  would have needed ~40GB and OOM'd mid-run. Fixed: only the downsized few-shot 
  images are cached (those are genuinely reused on every page); full-res renders
  are once-per-page and now recomputed. Verified, and logged as PLAYBOOK §3.10
  ("it worked on the pilot" is a statement about the pilot's size, not the
  design).

  🟡 Found & fixed: documentation drift

  - RESUME_HERE.md was dangerously stale — it still instructed a future session
  to resume the overlap-resolver work (finished + dropped). Rewritten to current
  state.
  - Auto-memory likewise pointed at the dead in-progress task — updated.
  - auto_labeler.py usage docstring didn't mention --concurrency — added.
  - Editor docstring described only the old accept/dismiss queue — now documents
  Extend/Add, persisted dismissals, ghost preview, zoom/pan, Merge ▲/Split ▼,
  and the key bindings.

  🟢 Audited & sound (no action)

  - All 9 tools compile + import clean; no stale refs or dead variables from the
  refactors; argparse coherent.
  - Concurrency thread-safety: per-page output dirs (no write collisions), token
  totals aggregated in the main thread, usage.csv written once at the end,

● Yes — your instinct was exactly right, and it was worse than step_4's version.
  Full findings:

  What step_1 had (both halves of the disease)

  1. convert_from_path(pdf_path) — rendered the whole volume into RAM at once
  (~437MB/page at your configured 300 DPI)
  2. cleaned_images — accumulated every completed page until one final
  save(append_images=…) — precisely the "caches completed files and holds them"
  you observed

  Volume_4 (273 pages, your current FILES_TO_RUN) would have needed >100GB —
  hence the swap-thrashing weirdness.

  Fixed (checkpoint 10_step1-oom-fix has the original)

  - Streamed chunks (PAGES_PER_CHUNK = 8): render chunk → clean chunk (pool
  reused across chunks) → write chunk-PDF → free → next; final stitch via
  pdfunite (poppler — already a dependency, zero new packages)
  - Bonus: per-chunk progress logging (cleaned pages 17–24 / 52) — the old code
  was silent for an entire volume

  Validated on real data: Appendix_1, 52/52 pages, output renders correctly,
  cleaning quality identical (verified visually) — peak memory 363MB, 4.6s (vs
  ~7.6GB the old way at test DPI; at 300 DPI the difference is ~30GB → ~1.5GB).

  Bonus bug found while testing 🐛

  main() resolved source_data one directory too shallow (pipeline_v2/source_data
  instead of project root, where your config docs — and your actual folder —
  put it). As the code stood, running step_1 today would find 0 PDFs and exit 
  "successfully" without doing anything. Fixed to match the documented location.

  Both lessons are logged in PLAYBOOK §3.10 (the grep-able pattern:
  convert/load(whole file) + results.append(…) + single save at end; and "an
  empty work list is almost never success — be loud").

  Your existing dpi_150_cleaned_pdfs/ outputs were untouched (test wrote to
  /tmp). Volume_4 at 300 DPI is now genuinely runnable whenever you want it. And
  Appendix_3 remains GO.

● While that runs, the corrected math that fits your symptom: at 300 DPI (your
  current TARGET_DPI, and likely what you were running when it crashed) a letter
  page is ~25MB RGB, so Volume_2's working set alone is ~13–16GB. One volume
  per process fits in 30GB ✓. But multi-volume in one process stacks that on the
  retained ratchet we just measured (which would be ~4× bigger at 300 DPI than
  the 2.3GB we saw at 150) — so the later volumes in the run are the ones that
  breach RAM. Crash late, restart one-at-a-time works. That's your exact
  experience.

      194  Geometric QA sees coverage + overlap + schema. It can NEVER se
           e: wrong category on a

● So you were right to push back — here's the full decoded story:

  Why one-at-a-time never crashed: a single volume's working set fits — even at
  your configured 300 DPI it's ~13–16GB on a 30GB machine. Each fresh process
  starts clean, pays its own peak, exits. ✓

  Why multi-volume runs crashed late: two stacked effects, now both measured:
  1. The accumulation (the thing you noticed): the old code held the entire
  rendered volume plus every completed cleaned page until the final save.
  2. The ratchet (the thing that made it late): in one long-lived process, that
  memory is logically freed between volumes but never returns to the OS — we
  measured RSS climbing 54 → 2,263MB across 7 volumes at 150 DPI with zero leaks
  in the code, pure allocator retention. At 300 DPI everything is 4× bigger, so
  volume N runs on top of an ever-taller floor until a later volume's 13–16GB
  peak no longer fits → OOM-kill, late in the run. Restart one-at-a-time = fresh
  floor every time = fine. That's your fingerprint exactly. (If "more than one
  at once" meant parallel terminals rather than a multi-file FILES_TO_RUN, the
  math is even simpler — two 300-DPI working sets are additive — but both
  versions land in the same place.)

  What the fix guarantees: memory is now bounded by the chunk (8 pages), not the
  volume, the DPI, or how many volumes you queue — measured flat ~500MB across 
  all seven volumes in one process, and it would stay in the low GBs even at 300
  DPI for all volumes back-to-back. The whole story (symptom → measurement →
  mechanism → rules) is logged in PLAYBOOK §3.10, including the diagnostic
  heuristic: "crashes only late / only in combination" is the ratchet's 
  fingerprint.

  Test artifacts cleaned up; step_1 is back to just the fixed pdf_cleaner.py (+
  the source-path fix). You can now safely re-run all volumes at 300 DPI in a
  single command if you want — and Appendix_3 in step_4 is still ready on your
  word.
